    # The logic of `SurfaceHolderCallback` will mostly be retained when converting to Compose, since [we'll utilise `AndroidExternalSurface`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/AndroidExternalSurface.composable#AndroidExternalSurface(androidx.compose.ui.Modifier,kotlin.Boolean,androidx.compose.ui.unit.IntSize,androidx.compose.foundation.AndroidExternalSurfaceZOrder,kotlin.Boolean,kotlin.Function1)). -https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48251	(@Narfinger, @mrobinson, #48251)	script_bindings: Enforce no_gc for lifetime of byte array in DOMString calls (#48251)
    ^ commit 8ff539803ee316c63d18c52a00e6ff4eb5d9e03a
    # As discussed in this zulip thread (https://servo.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/263398-general/topic/Is.20domstring.3A.3Aget_latin1_string.20unsafe.3F/with/625372322), get_latin1_string had unsafe behavior. If the bytes array is kept alive while a GC happens, we would point to potentially undefined memory.
    # This fixes this by bounding the lifetime of the `EncodedBytes` struct with the lifetime of a NoGC token.
    # For most of the methods we create a new NoGC token, as most of the uses do not escape the function and, hence, will not make a GC happen (as the spidermonkey function called enforces a similar NoGC token).
    # We add comments to clarify the requirements and why this behavior is safe.
    # 
    # In the codebase we had this unsafe behavior once in script/dom/fetch/request.rs in constructor method. Because of the reason Headers() function is used, this was never unsafe.
    # 
    # Testing: DOMString has unit tests and WPT will find anything else but this is mostly a change to lifetimes and internal API.
    #

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48577	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #48577)	build: bump btleplug from 0.13.2 to 0.13.3 (#48577)
    ^ commit 14648eb8933ce0aa9ad79a6ec57675f08237b0b7
    # Bumps [btleplug](https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug) from 0.13.2 to 0.13.3.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">btleplug's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>0.13.3 (2026-09-27)</h1>
    # <h2>Bugfixes</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix a race between waking and closing Android (<code>droidplug</code>) JNI adapter
    # closures that could free a closure while another thread was still calling
    # it. Concurrent wake and close were enough to trigger SIGSEGV/SIGBUS crashes
    # through <code>fn_adapter_call_internal</code>, which dominated the native crash rate of
    # Android apps using btleplug. Closures are now reference counted in the
    # adapter object's handle field, and wake/close access to that field is
    # serialized on the object monitor, so an in-flight call always holds its own
    # reference.</li>
    # <li>Fix CoreBluetooth <code>discover_services()</code> never resolving when service or
    # characteristic discovery reported an error, when the peripheral had no
    # services, or when the peripheral was no longer known to the adapter. Service
    # discovery errors are now returned, and a characteristic discovery error
    # completes that service without characteristics. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/486">#486</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix CoreBluetooth panics on late or unexpected discovery callbacks, such as
    # characteristics for included services or descriptors arriving after discovery
    # finished. The panic killed the CoreBluetooth event thread, so every later
    # operation on the adapter hung. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/487">#487</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix concurrent CoreBluetooth <code>connect()</code>, <code>disconnect()</code>, or
    # <code>discover_services()</code> calls on one peripheral leaving all but the last caller
    # waiting forever. Every concurrent caller now receives the result. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/488">#488</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix CoreBluetooth reads, writes, subscriptions, and descriptor operations
    # never resolving when services were rediscovered, invalidated by the device, or
    # when peripherals were cleared. Rediscovery keeps in-flight operations on
    # attributes that still exist, operations on attributes that disappear fail with
    # an error, and a services-modified event only drops the invalidated services.
    # On CoreBluetooth, <code>Central::clear_peripherals()</code> now disconnects any
    # peripheral that is still connected. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/489">#489</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix a Windows deadlock when GATT operations ran concurrently on
    # characteristics of the same service, for example two <code>subscribe()</code> calls
    # joined on one task. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/481">#481</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix a Windows scanner crash when a nearby device advertised a truncated
    # service data section. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/482">#482</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix 128-bit service data UUIDs being reported byte-reversed on Windows. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/483">#483</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix every GATT operation failing on Windows after reconnecting a device whose
    # connection dropped, until <code>disconnect()</code> was called explicitly. <code>connect()</code> on
    # an already connected peripheral now returns immediately. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/484">#484</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix BlueZ <code>mtu()</code> panicking on BlueZ versions that do not report the MTU
    # (older than 5.62). The panic also poisoned the peripheral's service cache,
    # breaking every later operation on it. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/485">#485</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix a failed Android service discovery never resolving <code>discover_services()</code>
    # and permanently blocking the peripheral's command queue. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/491">#491</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix an Android disconnect during a descriptor read or write, MTU request, or
    # RSSI read permanently blocking the peripheral's command queue and suppressing
    # <code>DeviceDisconnected</code>. Any operation interrupted by a disconnect now fails with
    # <code>Error::NotConnected</code>; previously writes could report success and reads or
    # discovery failed with an unrelated JNI error. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/490">#490</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <!-- raw HTML omitted -->
    # </blockquote>
    # <p>... (truncated)</p>

    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rustls/tokio-rustls/releases">tokio-rustls's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>0.26.6</h2>
    # <p>0.26.5 added an optimization to batch multiple reads which could swallow some errors. Buffer errors so that they propagate to the caller as soon as the caller next reads from the stream.</p>
    # <h2>What's Changed</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Buffer and propagate errors from transport by <a href="https://github.com/dtolnay"><code>@​dtolnay</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/tokio-rustls/pull/205">rustls/tokio-rustls#205</a></li>
    # <li>Prepare 0.26.6 by <a href="https://github.com/djc"><code>@​djc</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rustls/tokio-rustls/pull/206">rustls/tokio-rustls#206</a></li>
    # </ul>

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48579	(@dependabot[bot], @dependabot[bot], #48579)	build: bump wl-clipboard-rs from 0.9.3 to 0.9.4 (#48579)
    ^ commit 8403f210613ee2811009b9810fb3a529d88bca61
    # Bumps [wl-clipboard-rs](https://github.com/YaLTeR/wl-clipboard-rs) from 0.9.3 to 0.9.4.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/YaLTeR/wl-clipboard-rs/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">wl-clipboard-rs's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>v0.9.4 (28th Sep 2026)</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Copy no longer reports an error when the paste destination closes its pipe too early.</li>
    # <li>Added a <code>watch</code> module that provides a blocking API for monitoring clipboard changes. Create a <code>Watcher</code>, then loop on <code>while let Some(event) = watcher.next_event()? {}</code>. The event contains the new MIME types and lets you receive the clipboard contents if you wish.</li>
    # <li>Added <code>wl-paste --watch &lt;COMMAND&gt;</code> that works like the C wl-clipboard version. The command is invoked with the new clipboard contents on stdin, and <code>CLIPBOARD_STATE</code> and <code>CLIPBOARD_TYPE</code> environment variables set.</li>
    # <li>Added <code>wl-copy --sensitive</code> and <code>copy::Options::sensitive()</code> to hint to clipboard managers that copied data is sensitive.</li>
    # <li>Improved handling of <code>x-kde-passwordManagerHint</code>: it is offered last, requests for it do not count toward <code>serve_requests</code>, and automatic-type pastes prefer other MIME types.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48571	(@mxmgorin, #48571)	webgl: Initialize framebuffers before blitFramebuffer (#48571)
    wip; webgl
    ^ commit 8bdd959d652373831255d260b27dd6dfd4989d52
    # This makes `blitFramebuffer` initialize the attachments of both framebuffers before blitting, as required for [WebGL resources](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/1.0/#:~:text=must%20always%20contain%20initialized%20data), and generate `INVALID_FRAMEBUFFER_OPERATION` when either framebuffer is incomplete, as required by [OpenGL ES 3.0 spec](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf) (4.3.3). Previously the destination was cleared at the next read, discarding the blit, so every multisample resolve read back zeros, and an uninitialized source was copied as is.
    # 
    # Testing: `multisample-with-full-sample-counts.html` in `conformance2/` now pass.
    # 
-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48017	(@mrobinson, #48017)	constellation: Move `BrowsingContextGroup` to `ConstellationWebView` (#48017)
    ^ commit 1a6ef53a2969f18fa30712ecfe82abd886b55560
    # This is the first simplification from #47294. It moves
    # `BrowsingContextGroup` to `ConstellationWebView`. Every WebView has a
    # single `BrowsingContextGroup`, but this group might be shared between
    # `WebView`s. This is enforced by keeping groups in an `Rc<RefCell<..>>`
    # -- the shared ownership reflecting the reality of the specification.
    # 
    # In addition, this change fixes a leak of `WebGPU` threads that happened
    # when a `WebView` was destroyed before shutdown.
    # 
    # Testing: This should not change behavior (other than the leak fix, which
    # is difficult to test) so is covered by existing tests.
    # Fixes: This is part of #47294.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48599	(@mrobinson, #48599)	script: Do not consider `width` a relevant `<image>` mutation (#48599)
    dom; bugfix
    ^ commit 2e584dbf9d1db78e19a3e65c15c519bc2088d95e
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # The specification says that `width` should be considered a relevant
    # mutation (i.e. one that can trigger new image data loading), but no
    # other browser does this. This was probably added to the specification
    # because it used to be used in a `srcset` fallback. No browser

    # Similar to #48416 but for stop navigation. It's debatable whether `stop()` should live in `ServoNavigator` or not, since `ServoNavigator` is modelled off of the [Navigation API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigation), and `stop()` doesn't exist on it. If we're following the web's lead, it should exist on `Window`, but that's not really a concept in Android at the moment. .https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48485	(@webbeef, #48485)	layout: Layout flex items once when their cross size is known upfront (#48485)
    perf; less work
    ^ commit b0c20e94ae274e4b0858209c74f92a5675d86ce7
    #  In a single-line flex container with a definite cross size, the cross
    #  size of the flex line is the inner cross size of the container, so it's
    #  known before laying out the items. Still, each item was first laid out to
    #  find its hypothetical cross size, and stretched items were then laid out
    #  again with their used cross size.
    # 
    # Here instead, when the used cross size of an item doesn't depend on
    # its layout, we compute it upfront and lay the item out with it directly.
    # The final phase still checks the used cross size and lays the item out
    # again if it differs. Items in orthogonal flows keep the previous behavior.
    # 
    # Testing: no regression in WPT tests
    # 
    # Note: this is my first layout patch and it took me many many attempts to
    # not regress WPT and testing with casual browsing. This change improves
    # the speedometer score from 11.6 to 13.9 on my machine. I hope it's
    # correct but this needs careful review!

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48640	(@jdm, #48640)	script: Prioritize converting JS values to interfaces (ie. typed arrays) over sequences. (#48640)
    perf; https://rydmike.com/flexcolorscheme/themesplayground-latest/ impacted
    ^ commit 4868cd690f458b723321ef29e9f8e9d1335ed21d
    # The changes in this PR allow us to match the same logic in Gecko (https://searchfox.org/firefox-main/rev/b4b9efb6ee130740a94caf46ffff0625af1d51db/dom/bindings/Codegen.py#13131-13153). This matters because for unions like `Float32ArrayOrUnrestrictedFloatSequence`, trying to convert a typed array to a float sequence succeeds but has terrible performance; prioritizing interface conversions works because the WebIDL parser treats builtin types that are typed arrays as interfaces. Our implementation is different than Gecko because we're not using explicit if/else; just match blocks with failure cases that fall through to the next conversion attempt.
    # 
    # Testing: Should only be a performance optimization; any behaviour differences should be caught be existing tests.
    # Fixes: #48633
    # Fixes: #48537
    #

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48651	(@mxmgorin, #48651)	webgl: Read WebGL 2 *_BITS from the draw framebuffer attachments (#48651)
    wip; webgl
    ^ commit a87bf2ca96013dd58f3d423270f431a9f66fe19a

    # This makes WebGL 2 `getParameter` read `*_BITS` from the draw framebuffer's attachments, as described in [ES 3.0 (4.4.5)](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#nameddest=subsection.4.4.5).     # This is a minor benefit, but before when the `mediaSession` was only initialised when a media session had started, the user could only customise the media notifications (via the Servo app settings at the system level, not the in-app settings) if they had already played some media. If not then it'll look like Servo doesn't produce any notifications as no notification channels will be visible. With this change, the user can customise the notifications before ever seeing a notification.
    #

    # This makes `drawBuffers([BACK])` and `readBuffer(BACK)` on the default framebuffer use `COLOR_ATTACHMENT0`, and makes buffer swaps discard a pending GL error instead of panicking. Previously GL rejected `BACK` for the FBO used by the canvas, so these calls had no effect. In release builds the error also stayed pending until the swap, where surfman failed to create the next surface on macOS.

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48372	(@sagudev, @jdm, #48372)	webgpu: new buffer content paradigm (#48372)
    wip; webgpu
    ^ commit 42edcf82ff8dae13d0a808726b2e0ac359dbd50f
    # First commit just updates WebGPU expectations for main.
    # 
    # Per https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10307 it is actually not safe to read ptr returned from get_mapped_range of write only buffer. The idea of spec-authors is that one has content side buffer which preserves content if buffer is write only mapped. That paradigm is used by firefox and will soon also be used by deno.
    # 
    # Firefox uses persistent shared memory for content side buffer, which is allocated for whole buffer size, but populated only for mapped range. We do similarly, but we explicitly pass ownership from script to WGPU thread and back (reuse same shared memory). At any point shared memory handle is alive in at most one thread (in single threaded mode where we do not use actual IPC this is enforced by [`Arc::get_mut`](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/sync/struct.Arc.html#method.get_mut)). This subsumes https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/34071
    # 
    # This PR also adds&updates spec text and aligns code to spec better.
    # 
    # TL;DR: this PR gives us both performance and correctness.
    # 
    # Testing: CTS and manual performance testing using https://sagudev.github.io/briefcase/webgpu_map_speed.html?write=true  (firefox has ~100ms for both read/write, because that's their polling interval; servo has ~85ms on read and ~420ms on write in 2026-09-26 daily build and ~8ms for both read/write with this PR).
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48671	(@yezhizhen, #48671)	script: Dispatch touch compatibility mouse events synchronously in `ScriptThread` (#48671)
    dom; bugfix
    ^ commit 53f2a80314ba80e40a33cca4bd883fd1a2dfb026
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Compatibility mouse events for a tap were synthesized by the paint thread as new 
    # input events after confirmed by script->constellation->embedder->paint, 
    # so they were processed on a later rendering tick than the touchend that triggered them, making touch tests racy/flaky.
    # One particular serious case is, the hit-test for compat mouse events happened when the layout state has already changed.
    # 
    # This simplifies API as well, by removing unused `render_api` in `notify_input_event_handled` and point in `PendingClick`.
    # It also simplifies messaging: `simulate_mouse_click` is now removed from paint, which was like
    # paint -> constellation -> script -> embedder -> paint, and repeated 3 times as we had duplicate hit-test 3 times!
    # 
    # Testing: `mouseevents-after-touchend.tentative.html` and `/touch-events/hover-state-caused-by-compatibility-mouse-events.tentative.html` has new passing and no longer intermittent.
    # WebDriver `perform_actions/pointer_touch.py` has 4 more new failures: this is newly filed #48619 which has
    # always been there, but previously hidden.
    # 
    # Fixes: https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/36957#pullrequestreview-2831267053, performance related concern
    # Fixes: #40233, incorrect behaviour
    # ### Intermittent ones
    # Fixes: https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/46497
    # Fixes: #42813
    # Fixes: https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/46781
    # Fixes: https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/46277
    # Fixes: #41175
    # Fixes: #44693

    # Currently, some code uses the following pattern:
    # `let attr_string = elem.get_attribute_string_value(local_name!("bar")).unwrap_or_default()`.
    # Returning either the attribute value or an empty string.
    # 
    # We want to support this functionality on `AttrStrRef` to keep the flow of code similar with changes from `get_attribute_string_value` to `get_attribute_string_ref`.
    # 
    # This implements a way to support this code by returning an empty AttrRef.
    # 
    # Testing: Currently there are no automatic tests for this.
    # 
.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48678	(@mxmgorin, #48678)	webgl: Fix vertexAttribIPointer and the attribute type check (#48678)

    ^ commit aa92a88c32569964a52935a38cb5c9291d4efc59
    # This makes `vertexAttribIPointer` reach GL and checks attribute types against the shader based on which `vertexAttrib*` call set them, as described in the [spec](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/2.0/#ATTRIBUTE_TYPE_MATCH). Previously integer attributes received wrong values, and non-`FLOAT` `vertexAttribPointer` data flagged `INVALID_OPERATION` on every WebGL 2 draw. It also makes `getVertexAttrib` report `VERTEX_ATTRIB_ARRAY_INTEGER`.

    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>

    # <li>sync: fix mpsc index wraparound in block reclamation (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/tokio-rs/tokio/issues/8546">#8546</a>)</li>

    # and are re-exported from <code>audio_session_manager</code>.</li>

    # <li><code>bundle</code> — <code>ohos-libbundle-sys</code> (BundleManager / native bundle NDK,
    # <code>libbundle_ndk.z.so</code>): application identity (<code>bundleName</code>, <code>appId</code>,
    # <code>appIdentifier</code>, fingerprint, compatible device type, debug-mode flag),
    # main-element discovery, module metadata, and the API-21 file-type intent
    # discovery flow (<code>OH_NativeBundle_GetAbilityResourceInfo</code>).</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Changed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>xcomponent 0.4.0: the <code>keyboard-types</code> feature now covers the full key-event
    # translation (<code>Code</code>, <code>NamedKey</code>, <code>Location</code>, US-layout characters and a
    # stateful <code>KeyEventConverter</code>), and linking <code>ace_ndk.z</code> is limited to
    # OpenHarmony targets so the crate's tests run on the host.
    # The crate MSRV is raised to 1.85.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>v0.9.0</h2>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <p>New sub-crates, each also re-exported as an umbrella feature on <code>ohos-sys</code>:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li><code>accesstoken</code> — <code>ohos-accesstoken-sys</code> (AbilityAccessControl / AccessToken NDK)</li>
    # <li><code>asset-store</code> — <code>ohos-asset-store-sys</code> (AssetStoreKit)</li>
    # <li><code>basic-services-kit</code> — <code>ohos-basic-services-kit-sys</code>, with sub-features
    # <code>commonevent</code>, <code>battery-info</code>, <code>print</code>, <code>scan</code>, <code>os-account</code>, <code>time-service</code></li>
    # <li><code>crypto</code> — <code>ohos-crypto-sys</code> (CryptoArchitectureKit)</li>
    # <li><code>huks</code> — <code>ohos-huks-sys</code> (Universal Keystore Kit)</li>
    # <li><code>ipckit</code> — <code>ohos-ipckit-sys</code></li>
    # <li><code>locationkit</code> — <code>ohos-locationkit-sys</code></li>
    # <li><code>media</code> — <code>ohos-media-sys</code> (Player / Recorder / Transcoder, AVCodec,
    # demuxer / muxer, screen capture, low-power AV sinks; sub-crate existed
    # previously, this release adds the umbrella re-export)</li>
    # <li><code>netmanager</code> / <code>net_ssl</code> / <code>netstack</code> — NetworkKit subsystems

    # Mirrors changes from https://github.com/jschwe/ServoDemo/pull/32/changes. See that PR for the individual commits (DevEco Studio refuses to open the vendored project in this repo, since it only opens folders with a DevEco project at the git root, so for changes like this it's much easier to work in the IDE in the ServoDemo and mirror the changes back here).
    # 
    # Main changes:
    # 
    # - Give each XComponent a unique ID, instead of the same name.
    # - `Tabs` apparently destroys and rebuilds all entries when removing one entry, which using `Stack` avoids.
    # - Use a monotonic counter for the tab ids, to avoid any potential issues with id reuse.
    # - In a future PR, we would completely switch to id, and remove usage of index, but that requires changes on the Rust side, so I'm splitting that out.
    # - Fix deprecation warnings
    # - Fix warning about missing try-catch block
    # - formatting changes

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48715	(@yug105, #48715)	script: Replace nullable mutable DOM pointers with MutNullableDom (#48715)
    ^ commit 550373841fa21a6e238d38d3c3ace428b5d35a5e
    # Several DOM objects stored a nullable, mutable pointer as `DomRefCell<Option<Dom<T>>>`. I replaced these with `MutNullableDom<T>`, which is already made for this use case and removes the `.borrow()`, `.as_ref()`, and `.map(DomRoot::from_ref)` boilerplate. There are 11 fields in 8 files. The affected structs are Window, AudioTrack, VideoTrack, TextTrack, VTTCue, ServiceWorkerRegistration, TextInputWidgetShadowTree, and GPUQueue.
    # 
    # TextInputWidgetShadowTree derived `Clone`, but `MutNullableDom` does not implement `Clone`, so I removed the derive since nothing was using it and the code still builds. GPUQueue::set_device also no longer needed its `no_gc` parameter because it was only used by `safe_borrow_mut`, so I removed it and updated the one caller in gpudevice.rs. Both changes were discussed on Zulip with Narfinger and jdm: https://servo.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/263398-general/topic/MutNullableDom/with/629544110
    # 
    # Testing: There is no behavior change, so I did not add new tests. I ran `./mach test-wpt dom/events` and got the same result before and after: 214 expected, plus 3 unexpected `scrollend` passes that were already there. The `webvtt` video tests time out locally because my build uses `--media-stack dummy`, and the 2 region tests also hang on my machine without the TextTrack/VTTCue changes.
    # 
    # Fixes: #48553

    ^ commit 4f597e9dd33fa5e4ef8fbe689a0e97b31260e9be
    # Bumps [libc](https://github.com/rust-lang/libc) from 0.2.189 to 0.2.190.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/libc/releases">libc's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>0.2.190</h2>
    # <p>There is now a single config for enabling 64-bit <code>time_t</code>: <code>libc_unstable_time64</code>. This can be set
    # unconditionally; it opts in to 64-bit <code>time_t</code> on the following platforms that use 32-bit by
    # default:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li>32-bit Linux-GNU</li>
    # <li>32-bit Linux-uClibc</li>
    # <li>32-bit Linux-musl. Note that setting this flag also enables some other changes that happend in
    # musl v1.2.</li>
    # <li>32-bit Windows-GNU</li>
    # <li>ESP-IDF (all targets with this environment are 32-bit)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <p>Most other 32-bit platforms are either already using 64-bit <code>time_t</code>, or are considered legacy
    # and will not be gaining support from their upstream maintainers.</p>
    # <p>You can enable this using <code>RUSTFLAGS</code>:</p>
    # <pre lang="sh"><code>RUSTFLAGS='--cfg=libc_unstable_time64' cargo ...
    # </code></pre>
    # <p>Note that there may still be some changes to features gated by this config option, hence &quot;unstable&quot;
    # in its name. In the near future we will rename it to just <code>libc_time64</code>. Until then, please test it
    # out and report any bugs you find!</p>
    # <h3>Support</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Add initial support for HelenOS (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/4355">#4355</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <p>We are slowly filling out the <code>Default</code> implementations, to reduce the need for <code>mem::zeroed()</code> in user code:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Unix: Implement <code>Default</code> for a number of structs, especially on Apple platforms (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5576">#5576</a>)</li>
    # <li>Linux, NetBSD: Give <code>statvfs</code> a <code>Default</code> impl (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5583">#5583</a>)</li>
    # <li>Linux: Add <code>Default</code> to a <code>linux/can.rs</code> structs (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5257">#5257</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <p>Other additions:</p>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Expose the <code>libc_unstable_time64</code> cfg (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5411">#5411</a>)</li>
    # <li>Android, Glibc: Add <code>pthread_gettid_np</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5359">#5359</a>)</li>
    # <li>Android: Add <code>RTLD_NEXT</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5323">#5323</a>)</li>
    # <li>Android: Add reuseport BPF socket options (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5366">#5366</a>)</li>
    # <li>Apple: Add TCP header flags, options and SACK limits (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5358">#5358</a>)</li>
    # <li>Apple: Add <code>NET_RT_DUMP2</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5442">#5442</a>)</li>
    # <li>Apple: Add <code>posix_spawn_file_actions_add(f)chdir(_np)</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5558">#5558</a>)</li>
    # <li>BSD: Add <code>BPF_WORDALIGN</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5320">#5320</a>)</li>
    # <li>BSD: Add <code>lchmod</code> and <code>lchflags</code> where supported (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5400">#5400</a>)</li>
    # <li>BSD: Add <code>minherit</code> and related constants (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/4849">#4849</a>)</li>
    # <li>Docs: Add more links to public headers and manual pages (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5407">#5407</a>), (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/libc/pull/5485">#5485</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <!-- raw HTML omitted -->
    # </blockquote>
    # <p>... (truncated)</p>
    # </details>

    ^ commit 43fbdfbb32f7244a06440c3f5ea636f42c07de06
    # Bumps [btleplug](https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug) from 0.13.3 to 0.13.4.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">btleplug's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>0.13.4 (2026-10-03)</h1>
    # <h2>Behavior Changes</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li><strong>Windows Coded PHY scanning is now opt-in</strong>: Scans no longer request the
    # Coded (long-range) PHY by default, restoring the scanning behavior from
    # before 0.13.0. Since 0.13.0, scans requested it on every adapter that reported
    # Coded PHY support, but some of those adapters then stopped delivering most 1M
    # PHY advertisements, so ordinary nearby devices were never discovered.
    # Applications that need devices advertising on the Coded PHY should call
    # <code>Adapter::set_use_coded_phy(true)</code> before <code>start_scan()</code>. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/493">#493</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>Features</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Add <code>Adapter::set_use_coded_phy()</code> on Windows to opt in to receiving
    # advertisements on the Coded PHY. It has no effect on adapters that do not
    # report Coded PHY support, where requesting it would stop scans from reporting
    # any advertisements. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/493">#493</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/5ed67d697a3979f1e2ec93551051f11251a5f6f9"><code>5ed67d6</code></a> doc: Update version/CHANGELOG for 0.13.4</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/3a1b30d1e9219a4903a7a6cfbd08763a4fec9c66"><code>3a1b30d</code></a> test(corebluetooth): Cover unrequested descriptor-driven rediscovery</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/1543eb8b2a7ad11aea01bff4643b1bef56d8ef61"><code>1543eb8</code></a> doc: Fix rustdoc link errors in Error conversion and WinRT docs</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/bc4b1296396595bbc2c805dcf0c01f10fb8b6098"><code>bc4b129</code></a> fix(winrtble): Disable Coded PHY scanning by default</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/7a8397b58295b9d1444cade1e12b42a79dbfe359"><code>7a8397b</code></a> fix(winrtble): Keep the Coded PHY toggle behind the adapter support check</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/aa402e5e8c7cbd2e774a924f5e67113d97c53402"><code>aa402e5</code></a> feat(winrtble): allow overriding Coded PHY support</li>
    # <li>See full diff in <a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/compare/0.13.3...0.13.4">compare view</a></li>

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48714	(@jdm, #48714)	script: Prevent post-parsing stylesheet tasks from running on unloaded documents. (#48714)
    stability; crash fix for https://www.laika.com/our-films/wildwood
    ^ commit fd59630e03e4a569d0df55fd32931f719008d49e
    # The off-thread stylesheet parsing enqueues a task to run post-parse work on the main script thread, but it does so without using the document's task manager. This means that the task will always run unconditionally, even if the document is unloaded before the task runs. By using the task manager, the task will be instead be cancelled when this happens.
    # 
    # Testing: Can't write a deterministic test for this—the window in which the document must be aborted is between the network response finishing and before the stylesheet load event being fired, while the stylesheet source is being parsed on another thread. Refreshed https://www.laika.com/our-films/wildwood a bunch of times without triggering the original panic.
    # Fixes: #48345
    # 
    # 
    #

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47720	(@Narfinger, #47720)	script: Put PolicyContainer in document into an Arc (#47720)
    perf; allocations
    ^ commit 0a2e11ea26158dc180bafc7d45dcd294738dc5c0
    # As the PolicyContainer gets copied a bunch of time, we put it into a Reference Counted structure. As the PolicyContainer is also shared with net/ we need an Arc.
    # This reduces the allocations 1.6 million to 0.4 million over a roughly 1 minute browser session. We keep the return value of Ref<'_ Arc<PolicyContainer>> to always make the cloning specific.
    # Additionally, we make extensive use of Arc::make_mut which should almost always succeed.
    # 
    # Testing: This just changes the performance characteristics.
    #

    ^ commit 4ffea1cc6ae0e11d07e2eecbadde16629b1a1c0e
    # This makes `DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT` an alias for the depth and stencil attachment points in WebGL 2, as described in [spec](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/2.0/#FBO_ATTACHMENTS). +https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47933	(@mrobinson, #47933)	layout: Create separate stacking contexts for elements that establish 3d stacking contexts (#47933)
    layout; bugfix
    ^ commit 781cf6854eb29a7a629fe2f44cd5c763aec89c9b
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Gecko separates stacking contexts for dealing with `transform-style:
    # preserve-3d` and those that apply things like filters. Filters applied
    # to stacking contexts prevent them from participating in 3d rendering
    # contexts. This means that we must explicitly track when a stacking
    # context participates in a 3d rendering context and, in those cases, push
    # a separate WebRender stacking context for them.
    # 
    # Testing: This change fixes a WPT test.
    # Fixes: #47750.
    # 
.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48752	(@jschwe, #48752)	script: Fix TimerListener leak (#48752)
    perf; memory leak for timers
    ^ commit 5f65837a45d56f857c099e8c640dbf317ab4a0d1
    # Fixes a leak, where the Trusted reference would keep windows alive until the Timer expires.
    # Looking up the global via the pipelineId avoids this. For workers we can keep the direct reference since the timer scheduler is owned by the worker, hence if that is dropped, so is the reference.
    # 
    # Testing: We don't have automated tests to prevent leaks. 
    # Fixes: #48741

    # This makes `getUniformLocation` return `null` for members of a named uniform block, as described in [OpenGL ES 3.0 (2.12.6)](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/specs/es/3.0/es_spec_3.0.pdf#page=72).     # Servo had a hack where an empty text field always contained a zero-width
    # space to ensure that it created an inline formatting context. This
    # changes replaces that hack with a new method called
    # `text_node_needs_caret_placeholder()` which forces the creation of an
    # inline formatting context, the insertion of a caret placeholder for
    # empty text runs, and reserves the line height so that empty text inputs
    # are at least as tall as one line.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48755	(@mrobinson, #48755)	layout: Add support for the `<wbr>` element (#48755)
    layout; new feature
    ^ commit 60e4c92d49973c637286190144226f4ae666887f
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Servo previously had a non-functional user-agent stylesheet
    # implementation of `<wbr>` (that maybe worked in old layout). This change
    # replaces it with a box tree construction path that adds a zero-width
    # space to inline content when `<wbr>` is encountered. This is

    # Bumps [cc](https://github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs) from 1.5.1 to 1.6.0.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/releases">cc's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>cc-v1.6.0</h2>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>add <code>Build::create_archive</code> and <code>emit_link_directives</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1972">#1972</a>)</li>
    # <li>read the process environment once per <code>Build</code>, on first use (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1969">#1969</a>)</li>
    # <li>add <code>Build::message_logger</code> for structured access to cc's messages (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1967">#1967</a>)</li>
    # <li>add <code>CXXSTDLIB_STATIC</code> to link the C++ stdlib statically from outside (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1957">#1957</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>key the flag support cache on target, host, language and environment (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1965">#1965</a>)</li>
    # <li>forward each line of compiler stderr once and whole with <code>parallel</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1959">#1959</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Other</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>find compile commands by source file so tests pass with <code>parallel</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1973">#1973</a>)</li>
    # <li>add support for <code>armeb-unknown-linux-gnueabi</code> (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1958">#1958</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>

    # The immediate win is that `Servo` consumers no longer have to show/hide the keyboard themselves via the callback (though they do still have to write a bit of boilerplate for now). In the longer term, this removes the most stubborn of references to `ServoView`, ultimately making its removal in sight.
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48708	(@alice, #48708)	a11y: Set the focused node for AccessKit (#48708)
    accessibility
    ^ commit f2fe72df95d2c2c82532e521d78191a423aaee7a
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    # When updating the accessibility tree, pass the document's focused element in via `ReflowRequestAccessibility` and `AccessibilityContext`.
    # 
    # After the tree is up to date, find the corresponding `AccessibilityNode` for the focused element, and store its `NodeId` as the `AccessibilityTree`'s `focused_node_id`.
    # 
    # Then, set the `focus` field in the final `TreeUpdate` either to the `AccessibilityTree`'s `focused_node_id`, or the ID of the tree's root node if that is `None`.
    # 
    # Finally, the WebView's focused node is set to be the graft node connecting the WebView's tree with the web contents' tree.
    # 
    # Note: this does not include necessary work for the WebView's focused node to be exposed as the focused node for ServoShell. This requires more significant changes to ServoShell which are out of scope for this change, but which are planned.
    # 
    # Testing: New test added for moving focus via keypress and via JavaScript.
    # Fixes: Part of #46744

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48719	(@Loirooriol, #48719)	script_bindings: Get values of Stylo's preferences from Stylo (#48719)
    embedding; removed pref
    ^ commit 4b388ed8eaa8df0601e9153568d3b6610e48b82f
    ^ /!\ may contain changes to feature flags
    # If a CSS property is defined in Stylo with `servo_pref`, then Servo needs to check this preference in order to know whether to expose it in CSSOM.
    # 
    # The problem was that the preference was being checked as a Servo preference, not a Stylo preference. So to avoid a panic due to an unknown preference, it was necessary to create a Servo preference mapping the Stylo one.
    # 
    # For example, Servo had to add the `layout_flexbox_balance` pref because Stylo has the `flex-line-count` property behind `layout.flexbox.balance`.
    # 
    # This patch makes it so that Stylo preferences without a mapping Servo preference will correctly get their value from Stylo.
    # 
    # Testing: Not needed, no change in behavior, other than removing the unnecessary `layout_flexbox_balance` pref.
    # Fixes: #47824
    #

    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    ^ /!\ contains WebIDL changes! did we ship a new API?
    ^ /!\ may contain changes to feature flags
    # Hi, I'm a spec champion for the Container Timing feature and I wanted to add it to Servo (behind a flag). 
    # 
    # The Container Timing API enables monitoring when annotated sections of the DOM are displayed on screen and have finished their initial paint. I am one of the spec authors, and i've been helping this land in both [Chromium](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/container-timing-origin-trial) and [Firefox](https://phabricator.services.mozilla.com/D279373)
    # 
    # This PR implements the [Container Timing API](https://github.com/WICG/container-timing), following the same layout > paint > constellation pipeline already used for LCP.
    # 
    #   Steps for calculating Container Timing:
    # 
    #   1. When an element has the `containertiming` attribute, a [flag is propagated down through its descendants](https://github.com/jasonwilliams/servo/blob/403908fa99f47da3e18faaca88d2abdb01498cf7/components/script/dom/node/node.rs#L378-L387) (set on the Node/fragment) so layout can cheaply tell "is this inside a container-timing root" without walking back up the tree on every fragment.
    #   2. While building the display list, each painted text/image fragment that belongs to a container-timing root [reports its viewport-clipped size and rect to a PaintTimingHandler](https://github.com/jasonwilliams/servo/blob/403908fa99f47da3e18faaca88d2abdb01498cf7/components/layout/display_list/mod.rs#L706-L718), similar to how LCP candidates are collected.
    #   3. PaintTimingHandler keeps one running record per container root (keyed by a stable node ID, since the containertiming value itself is optional and not unique),  accumulating size/rect across paints, and tracks which containers were actually touched since the last hand-off.
    #   4. Layout [hands the finished per-container records to script](https://github.com/jasonwilliams/servo/blob/403908fa99f47da3e18faaca88d2abdb01498cf7/components/layout/layout_impl.rs#L1561-L1567) as part of the reflow result
    #   5. once webrender confirms that epoch composited, [it sends PaintMetricEvent::ContainerTiming(paint_time, ids)](https://github.com/jasonwilliams/servo/blob/403908fa99f47da3e18faaca88d2abdb01498cf7/components/paint/painter.rs#L585-L589) to script, which stamps the already-stored records with the real paint/first-render time and queues the finished PerformanceContainerTiming entries.
    # 
    # Testing:
    # - There are plenty of [Web Platform Tests](https://wpt.fyi/results/container-timing/tentative?label=master&label=experimental&aligned&q=container%20timing) for Container Timing. I have been testing the build against these as i have been going along, 19/29 tests pass.
    # - There are tests for some components, such as paintedRegion
    # 
    # Follow ups & investigation:
    # - `lastPaintedElement` selection picks by traversal order, not largest area
    # - [Tidy Up] The `effective_visual_size` check was [originally written for LCP](https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/43378), but is useful for Container Timing as well, we should make this API generic
    # 
-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48772	(@Amrit-04, #48772)	script_bindings: remove obsolete permanent JS roots (#48772)

    # This is required for computing dimensions of an `<img>` which tests expect to be available synchronously when adding a `<source>` to a `<picture>`. However, the spec didn't account for this, despite browsers running this logic synchronously:
    # https://github.com/whatwg/html/issues/8207
    #

    # Bumps [arbitrary](https://github.com/rust-fuzz/arbitrary) from 1.4.2 to 1.5.0.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rust-fuzz/arbitrary/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md">arbitrary's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>1.5.0</h2>
    # <p>Released 2026-10-05.</p>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Added support for deriving <code>Arbitrary</code> for types with raw identifiers.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Changed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Updated <code>derive_arbitrary</code> to <code>syn</code> 3. This raises the MSRV from 1.63.0 to
    # 1.71.0, which is the MSRV of <code>syn</code> 3.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fixed soundness when generating arrays.</li>
    # <li>Fixed nonzero integer generation to always produce a nonzero value.</li>
    # <li>Updated <code>derive_arbitrary</code> to include fixes for fully qualified paths.</li>

    # Updates `wgpu-types` from 30.0.0 to 30.0.1
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/releases">wgpu-types's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>v30.0.1</h2>
    # <h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
    # <h4>Vulkan</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Stop passing an un-waited fence to <code>vkAcquireNextImageKHR</code> on non-Windows platforms, which triggered <code>VUID-vkAcquireNextImageKHR-fence-10066</code> validation errors every frame since v30.0.0. By <a href="https://github.com/ErichDonGubler"><code>@​ErichDonGubler</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9855">#9855</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h4>Metal</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Resolve metal color space constants dynamically. By <a href="https://github.com/andyleiserson"><code>@​andyleiserson</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9819">#9819</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h4>WebGPU</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Upgrade vendored WebGPU bindings and <code>wasm-bindgen</code> to 0.2.127. This fixes a panic “<code>can't access property &quot;info&quot;, arg0 is null</code>” when using the WebGPU backend and <code>requestAdapter()</code> fails. By <a href="https://github.com/beicause"><code>@​beicause</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10034">#10034</a>, backported in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10105">#10105</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/blob/trunk/CHANGELOG.md">wgpu-types's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>v30.0.1 (2026-08-21)</h2>
    # <h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
    # <h4>Vulkan</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Stop passing an un-waited fence to <code>vkAcquireNextImageKHR</code> on non-Windows platforms, which triggered <code>VUID-vkAcquireNextImageKHR-fence-10066</code> validation errors every frame since v30.0.0. By <a href="https://github.com/ErichDonGubler"><code>@​ErichDonGubler</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9855">#9855</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h4>Metal</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Resolve metal color space constants dynamically. By <a href="https://github.com/andyleiserson"><code>@​andyleiserson</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9819">#9819</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h4>WebGPU</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Upgrade vendored WebGPU bindings and <code>wasm-bindgen</code> to 0.2.127. This fixes a panic “<code>can't access property &quot;info&quot;, arg0 is null</code>” when using the WebGPU backend and <code>requestAdapter()</code> fails. By <a href="https://github.com/beicause"><code>@​beicause</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10034">#10034</a>, backported in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10105">#10105</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>

    # Updates `wgpu-naga-bridge` from 30.0.0 to 30.0.1
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>

    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>v30.0.1</h2>
    # <h3>Bug Fixes</h3>
    # <h4>Vulkan</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Stop passing an un-waited fence to <code>vkAcquireNextImageKHR</code> on non-Windows platforms, which triggered <code>VUID-vkAcquireNextImageKHR-fence-10066</code> validation errors every frame since v30.0.0. By <a href="https://github.com/ErichDonGubler"><code>@​ErichDonGubler</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9855">#9855</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h4>Metal</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Resolve metal color space constants dynamically. By <a href="https://github.com/andyleiserson"><code>@​andyleiserson</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/9819">#9819</a>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h4>WebGPU</h4>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Upgrade vendored WebGPU bindings and <code>wasm-bindgen</code> to 0.2.127. This fixes a panic “<code>can't access property &quot;info&quot;, arg0 is null</code>” when using the WebGPU backend and <code>requestAdapter()</code> fails. By <a href="https://github.com/beicause"><code>@​beicause</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10034">#10034</a>, backported in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/pull/10105">#10105</a>.</li>

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47255	(@janeoa, #47255)	devtools: feature guard (#47255)
    embedding; binary size + new feature
    ^ commit a0d7a24dc0c41bd39079626e5b8d1f096acb8521
    ^ /!\ contains libservo or embedder_traits changes! does it affect our public API?
    ^ /!\ contains servoshell changes! does it affect the user experience?
    ^ /!\ contains WebIDL changes! did we ship a new API?
    ^ /!\ may contain changes to EXPERIMENTAL_PREFS
    # This PR feature guards the devtools
    # 
    # Just like in the https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/47200 I introduced a new feature `devtools`. It is now optional and requires `debugging` (under the same feature guard as a direct dependency). This feature guard preserves `console`.
    # 
    # PR also introduces a small `default_tooling = ["servo/default_tooling"]` along to the already present `default_web_features`
    # ```toml
    # ...
    # default_tooling = ["devtools"]
    # ...
    # ```
    # 
    # (Cargo.toml of servoshell:)
    # ```toml
    # default = ["default_without_allocator", "servo-allocator/use-jemalloc"]
    # default_without_allocator = ["bundled", "default_web_features", "default_tooling", "max_log_level", "js_jit"]
    # default_web_features = ["servo/default_web_features", "gamepad", "webgpu", "webxr"]
    # default_tooling = ["servo/default_tooling"]
    # ```
    # 
    # Testing: `./mach build --no-default-features --features bundled,gamepad,webgpu,max_log_level,js_jit`
    # 
    # Builds that pass:
    # - `./mach build --no-default-features --features bundled,gamepad,webgpu,max_log_level,js_jit`
    # - `./mach build`
    # - `./mach build --features devtools`
    # - `./mach build --features webgl`
    # 
    # Fixes: [47187](https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/47187)
    # 
    # P.S. I've left a bunch of "dead_code" warnings unaddressed. Could be a future PR candidate, but I guess the `production` profile would optimize them away anyway and them extra feature guards might cause more clutter in the default build?
    #

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48753	(@mrobinson, @Loirooriol, #48753)	layout: Treat preserved tabs as conditionally hanging under `white-space: pre-wrap` (#48753)
    layout; bugfix
    ^ commit d9389a3d3dc56e340c23bc78429044ac301cdfec
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # The [specification] says that preserved tabs must hang conditionally
    # when `white-space` is `pre-wrap`. This change does that by
    # incorporating this idea into inline content sizing, white space
    # trimming, and inline layout line breaking.
    # 
    # [specification]: https://www.w3.org/TR/css-text-3/#white-space-phase-2
    # 
    # Testing: This change adds a WPT test that fails in Firefox, but passes
    # in Chrome and Safari. In addition 4 other WPT tests start to pass.

    # As of the current specification, `insertCompositionText` is the only `beforeinput` event that cannot be canceled. This change makes it so that Servo reflects this. A weird side effect is that IMEs can insert text into text controls even when `preventDefault` is called on every `beforeinput`. This behavior is present in other browsers, except for WebKit, which implements a non-standard proposal to the Input Events 2 specification.

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48839	(@alice, @mrobinson, #48839)	script: Map ARIA role for computed AccessKit role (#48839)
    accessibility
    ^ commit 7c1c278bf606a209ebc1bcb50c1262f391e47526
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Address TODO in `Element::get_computed_role()` to more reliably map from the computed AccessKit role for the element to its equivalent ARIA role.
    # 
    # We already had a map from ARIA role to AccessKit role which is used in computing the AccessKit role based on the element's `role` attribute, as well as some minimal logic for computing some AccessKit roles based on element type. This change:
    # 
    # - Splits the logic for mapping supported ARIA role to AccessKit role into two steps: determine whether the ARIA role is supported, and then map the ARIA role to the AccessKit role.
    # - Adds mappings for ARIA roles which aren't yet supported, including roles which we currently map from element type but not from the `role` attribute.
    # - Adds logic to compute the reverse mapping, from AccessKit role to ARIA role, which can be used to compute the computed ARIA-equivalent role name from the computed AccessKit role.
    # - Updates `Element::get_computed_role()` to use the reverse mapping from AccessKit role to ARIA role to more reliably compute the ARIA-equivalent role for the computed AccessKit role for the element.
    # 
    # This fixes many WPT tests which had been failing only because we weren't computing the correct ARIA equivalent role from the AccessKit role.
    # 
    # Testing: Test expectations updated as appropriate for existing test coverage.
    # Fixes: Part of #47339, part of #47182.

    # depend on each other.

    # - Introduce `ThreadTrustedPromiseHelpers` so that we can operate trusted promise from `script_webcrypto`.

    # - Update the import path.

    # - Update the code related to trusted promise.

    # <p><strong>Full Changelog</strong>: <a href="https://github.com/hyperium/h2/compare/v0.4.19...v0.4.20">https://github.com/hyperium/h2/compare/v0.4.19...v0.4.20</a></p>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/hyperium/h2/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">h2's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>0.4.20 (October 6, 2026)</h1>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix counting reserved push promises against stream concurrency limits.</li>
    # <li>Fix automatic small DATA frame budget to update when the connection window changes at runtime.</li>
    # <li>Fix rejecting duplicate <code>content-length</code> headers with different values.</li>
    # <li>Fix counting refused streams towards the local reset limit.</li>
    # <li>Fix client to reject responses without a <code>:status</code> pseudo header.</li>
    # <li>Fix rare race condition during shutdown.</li>
    # <li>Fix rejecting GOAWAY frames with a non-zero stream identifier.</li>
    # <li>Optimize HPACK Huffman encoding and decoding.</li>
    # <li>Reduce size of HPACK Huffman encoding tables.</li>
    # <li>Optimize receive performance by reducing send buffer locking.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48952	(@mrobinson, #48952)	script: Fix a memory leak with module scripts (#48952)
    perf; leak
    ^ commit fb11f484542fd9e2af6357187827f94e8d93c727
    # Module scripts were storing a rooted copy of their `GlobalScope` via
    # `BaseScript`. Since this `BaseScript` was stored via `SetModulePrivate`
    # outside of where our tracing infrastructure could see it, it was never
    # collected. This led to a cyclical reference to the global. This change makes
    # it so that the global isn't stored in `BaseScript`, but is fetched via the
    # SpiderMonkey API always -- which already had access to it.
    # 
    # In addition, the changes here bring the shape of the code closer to the
    # one in the specification.
    # 
    # Testing: We have no automated memory leak testing, but automated testing
    # showed that a similar fix reduced retained memory per-`<iframe>` load
    # during Speedometer 3.1 by about 7.2 megabytes.
    # 
+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48942	(@Loirooriol, #48942)	layout: Implement the stretch quirks for `<html>` and `<body>` (#48942)
    layout; quirks mode
    ^ commit 5975d977d6e4c09c621d7c74478a826e6c972e91
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # In quirks mode, by default, the `<html>` should fill the viewport, and the `<body>` should fill the `<html>`. This is different than `stretch`, because if the contents are larger, they should be sized normally like for `fit-content`. Therefore, this patch adds a new `Size::QuirkyStretch`, which is only allowed as a preferred block size. Then we use it as the automatic block size when needed.
    # 
    # Spec links:
    # - https://quirks.spec.whatwg.org/#the-html-element-fills-the-viewport-quirk

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/39869	(@Loirooriol, #39869)	layout: Implement the percentage height calculation quirk (#39869)
    layout; quirks mode
    ^ commit 6bde716df28ac0ca81d4b4c161b7c66f22f89eba
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # https://quirks.spec.whatwg.org/#the-percentage-height-calculation-quirk
    # 
    # In quirks mode, when a percentage in the block axis would resolve against an indefinite size, instead of behaving as auto, it can sometimes iterate the containing block chain to try to find a definite basis.
    # 
    # This patch implements this behavior by adjusting the containing block established for the contents, so no iteration is actually needed.
    # 
    # Testing: various tests improve. These are the remaining failures in `/quirks/percentage-height-calculation.html`:
    #  - Unlike Gecko, we apply the quirk to `calc()` containing percentages. Blink and WebKit do the same, and the consensus in https://github.com/whatwg/quirks/issues/48 seems to go this way.
    #  - We follow the spec and only stretch a `<body>` when it's the [document's body element](https://quirks.spec.whatwg.org/#documents-body-element). So arguably some subtests are wrong, in fact one of them fails on all browsers.
    # 
    # `/css/css-flexbox/percentage-size-quirks-005.html` is now failing because we apply the quirk to `stretch`. Restricting it to percentages would add too much complexity. This is being discussed in https://github.com/w3c/csswg-drafts/issues/12421
    # 
    # Fixes: #23038
    # Part of https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/11709
    #

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48968	(@mrobinson, #48968)	script: Only try to stop animations for nodes that have ever animated (#48968)
    perf; speedup
    ^ commit dd0c3216c3b5b36aa2304617af69bcdbf822e565
    # For every node removal we were checking to see if it was in the list of
    # animating nodes in order to clean up animations. This change creates a
    # new `NodeFlags::HAS_EVER_ANIMATED` to only do those checks when a node
    # has ever participated in an animation. This avoids a two `RwLock` write
    # unlocks, several hash lookups, and a `RefCell` borrow for every node
    # removal.
    # 
    # Testing: This should eliminate about 2.4% of the time in an `innerHTML`
    # microbenchmark in Speedometer 3.1.

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48957	(@mrobinson, #48957)	script: Handle dropping `TrustedPromise` (#48957)
    perf; leak
    ^ commit 4ba7263c55803faf7a341dd7aeda81f82d23f6a8
    # When debugging memory leaks that appeared in automated testing with
    # Speedometer 3.1. We noticed that dropping `TrustedPromise` without
    # rooting ends up leaking it. This change makes it so that
    # `TrustedPromise` works more like `Trusted`. It adds a `Drop` handler
    # that removes the `TracedPromise` from the table when on the originating
    # thread and also code that cleans up promises without remaining
    # references when dropped on other threads.
    # 
    # This fixes an issue where fetches were leaking promises:
    # 
    # 1. `fetch()` creates a `TrustedPromise` when building a `FetchContext`
    #    [^1].
    # 2. `TrustedPromise` is used to resolve the promise [^2].
    # 3. The promise is wrapped in a new `TrustedPromise` [^3].
    # 4. That promise is then thrown away without being used or rooted.
    # 
    # Fetches that are still in progress when a pipeline exits still leak, but
    # that will be fixed in a followup change.
    # 
    # [^1]: https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/315a8a27e297d85ee8202d8d8680eb98cacb0978/components/script/fetch/fetch.rs#L365
    # [^2]: https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/315a8a27e297d85ee8202d8d8680eb98cacb0978/components/script/fetch/fetch.rs#L628
    # [^3]: https://github.com/servo/servo/blob/315a8a27e297d85ee8202d8d8680eb98cacb0978/components/script/fetch/fetch.rs#L682
    # 
    # Testing: This change adds an automated Servo-specific test with the
    # remaining failing case marked in expectations.
    # 
.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48972	(@mxmgorin, #48972)	webgl: Allow drawing to a framebuffer without a color attachment (#48972)
    wip; webgl
    ^ commit a37da32ff39164518bb1aa0b452bd1737da6df23
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # This change allows drawing and clearing a framebuffer with no color attachment, such as a depth-only framebuffer. Previously, these operations failed with `INVALID_OPERATION`. The WebGL specification requires this error only when reading from a missing attachment ([spec](https://registry.khronos.org/webgl/specs/latest/1.0/#READING_FROM_MISSING_ATTACHMENT)), so the check now applies only to `readPixels` and `copyTex[Sub]Image2D`. As a side effect, depth-only and stencil-only framebuffers are now lazily initialized.
    # 
    # Testing: More subtests of the multisampled depth and stencil renderbuffer initialization tests now pass. Added a new WPT test that covers drawing, clearing, and reading.
.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48969	(@mxmgorin, #48969)	layout: Reuse one glyph buffer across text fragments (#48969)
    perf; less work
    ^ commit 22e048831ff503d3456eddd8f7944f17f3d9a9c3
    # This makes display list construction reuse a single buffer across text fragments. Previously, `glyphs()` allocated a new `Vec<GlyphInstance>` for every text fragment, only to copy it into the WebRender display list.
    # 
    # In a benchmark that rebuilds the display list of a 4,800-word page every frame (about 4,900 text fragments and 28,000 glyphs), this removes about 8,600 allocations per build and reduces the median time of the `DisplayListBuilder::build` trace span from 1.14 ms to 0.87 ms (release build with `tracing-perfetto`).
    # 
    # Testing: No behavior changes; existing WPT tests cover this.

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48964	(@mrobinson, #48964)	script: No longer throttle `PerformanceTimeline` tasks (#48964)
    ^ commit fb2540e81614d4e2bddd6c210df28899ebd94596
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # The [specification] says:
    # 
    # > The performance timeline task queue is a low priority queue that, if
    # > possible, should be processed by the user agent during idle periods to
    # > minimize impact of performance monitoring code.
    # 
    # which means that this throttling is optional ("if possible").  In
    # addition to causing flakiness in the WPT tests, it seems like
    # neither WebKit nor Firefox are doing this. This change removes the
    # throttling code completely.
    # 
    # In addition, this removes the special behavior for Rendering tasks which
    # are currently completely unused -- so a special behavior is not
    # necessary.
    # 
    # [specification]: https://w3c.github.io/performance-timeline/#dfn-performance-timeline-task-source
    # 
    # Testing: This change fixes a WPT test and flakiness in others.
    # Fixes: #40819.
    # Fixes: #44408.

    ^ commit 864236d52c32928a9cc543d8a7f114cb42688710
    # Bumps [cc](https://github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs) from 1.6.0 to 1.7.0.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Release notes</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/releases">cc's releases</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2>cc-v1.7.0</h2>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>add <code>Tool::get_envs</code>, deprecate Tool::env and store tool env as <code>Arc&lt;OsStr&gt;</code> pairs (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1985">#1985</a>)</li>
    # <li>link <code>c++</code> when Clang uses libc++ (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1978">#1978</a>)</li>
    # <li>add <code>CC_MASM_ASM</code> and fall back to <code>llvm-ml</code> for MASM files (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1975">#1975</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><em>(target parsing)</em> for powerpc64-ibm-aix abi (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1989">#1989</a>)</li>
    # <li>ignore ASCII case in program name checks (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1981">#1981</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Other</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><em>(compile)</em> issue compilation cmds more frequently in parallel <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/issues/1997">#1997</a> <a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/issues/1998">#1998</a></li>
    # <li><em>(cache)</em> Optimize tool detection caching (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1995">#1995</a>)</li>
    # <li><em>(cache)</em> optimize EnvSnapshot Hash, PartialEq, Ord impl (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1994">#1994</a>)</li>
    # <li>Regenerate target info (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1988">#1988</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md">cc's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h2><a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/compare/cc-v1.6.0...cc-v1.7.0">1.7.0</a> - 2026-10-10</h2>
    # <h3>Added</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li>add <code>Tool::get_envs</code>, deprecate Tool::env and store tool env as <code>Arc&lt;OsStr&gt;</code> pairs (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1985">#1985</a>)</li>
    # <li>link <code>c++</code> when Clang uses libc++ (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1978">#1978</a>)</li>
    # <li>add <code>CC_MASM_ASM</code> and fall back to <code>llvm-ml</code> for MASM files (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1975">#1975</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Fixed</h3>
    # <ul>
    # <li><em>(target parsing)</em> for powerpc64-ibm-aix abi (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1989">#1989</a>)</li>
    # <li>ignore ASCII case in program name checks (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/rust-lang/cc-rs/pull/1981">#1981</a>)</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h3>Other</h3>

    ^ commit 789d0835cc2745bfa7741522daddba147cb6d7b5
    # Bumps [btleplug](https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug) from 0.13.4 to 0.13.5.
    # <details>
    # <summary>Changelog</summary>
    # <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">btleplug's changelog</a>.</em></p>
    # <blockquote>
    # <h1>0.13.5 (2026-10-10)</h1>
    # <h2>Bugfixes</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Fix Android (<code>droidplug</code>) notification streams losing notifications, or
    # stopping entirely while the polling thread spun at full CPU. The Java
    # notification queue removed items outside its lock while the Bluetooth
    # callback thread added to it, which could corrupt the queue so that every
    # later poll failed and the error was silently retried. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/497">#497</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix Android <code>init</code> failing with <code>failed to resolve Java class 'com/nonpolynomial/btleplug/android/impl/Adapter'</code> when called from a thread
    # attached from native code, such as Dioxus's <code>manganis</code>. The <code>Adapter</code> class is
    # now resolved through the thread's context class loader, like every other
    # btleplug class. The calling thread's context class loader must be the
    # application's. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/495">#495</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix CoreBluetooth memory growing without bound while scanning. The
    # CoreBluetooth thread never drained an autorelease pool, so Objective-C objects
    # autoreleased for every advertisement report, such as peripheral name
    # conversions, were held for the life of the process. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/494">#494</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix BlueZ <code>connect()</code> failing with <code>Service discovery timed out</code> for
    # peripherals that take longer than 5 seconds to resolve services, which is
    # common for unpaired devices that BlueZ has no service cache for. The timeout
    # is now 30 seconds. (<a href="https://redirect.github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/issues/496">#496</a>)</li>
    # <li>Fix BlueZ adapters that report a <code>bluetooth</code> modalias being missing from
    # <code>Manager::adapters()</code>.</li>
    # </ul>
    # <h2>Dependencies</h2>
    # <ul>
    # <li>Update <code>bluez-async</code> from 0.8.2 to 0.9.0.</li>
    # </ul>
    # </blockquote>
    # </details>
    # <details>
    # <summary>Commits</summary>
    # <ul>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/1af8299c291f24fca68dfb6b7ef1b5c0475892e1"><code>1af8299</code></a> doc: Update version/CHANGELOG for 0.13.5</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/025019802a08ff6914fdfc633b82a4068c798fc2"><code>0250198</code></a> fix(corebluetooth): Drain an autorelease pool per message on the CoreBluetoot...</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/8ff867c477cf11f3028e5bd3af6ceacc11d64a20"><code>8ff867c</code></a> build(bluez): Update bluez-async to 0.9.0</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/3fb1d8888d1e6e6b92e0cacc56e3845a51569468"><code>3fb1d88</code></a> build(droidplug): Allow jni-macros deprecation warning on Rust 1.99</li>
    # <li><a href="https://github.com/deviceplug/btleplug/commit/16e03b45a9d12a7d0893b8fc12dde823597a8988"><code>16e03b4</code></a> Share QueueStream helpers across stream tests</li>

-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49014	(@gabrielecabrini, #49014)	script: migrate borrow_mut to safe_borrow_mut in HTMLDialogElement (#49014)
    stability; part of https://github.com/servo/servo/issues/40600

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49032	(@mrobinson, #49032)	script: Ensure that captured fetch state is traced (#49032)
    perf; leak
    ^ commit 51d111766b2103fe1396047e4ea1393d3703c3cf
    # When launching a fetch via the `fetch()` method, various bits of state
    # need to be captured to complete the algorithm. Before these were
    # stored as `Trusted<..>` members on the `FetchContext`, but those
    # would leak if the `Document` was torn down during an ongoing fetch.
    # Instead, store this state on the `GlobalScope`'s fetch group and look it
    # up when responding to network events. This prevents the data from
    # leaking.
    # 
    # In addition, stop rooting `GlobalScope` for all `NetworkListener`s.
    # Instead rely on `GlobalScope::current` returning the appropriate global
    # and pass `GlobalScope` to all `NetworkListener` methods. This is
    # ensured by the `ScriptThread` and worker task queues. This fixes the
    # issue with `GlobalScope` only. There are still other leaks and a
    # follow-up change will fix this in a more general way, but this change is
    # a prerequisite.
    # 
    # Testing: This fixes a `Document` leak test.
    # 
-https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/48986	(@rogerkorantenng, #48986)	script_bindings: Add safe wrapper for JS_GetOwnPropertyDescriptorById (#48986)

+https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49024	(@nicoburns, #49024)	layout: Upgrade Taffy to v0.15.0 (#49024)
    layout; perf improvements, bug fixes, new calc feature for fit-content
    ^ commit 79ee827b6a5a60a40cab662223f555528571ebcc
    ^ /!\ contains changes to WPT expectations! it probably affects the web platform
    # Upgrades Servo to Taffy v0.15.0 ([release notes](https://github.com/DioxusLabs/taffy/releases/tag/v0.15.0)).
    # 
    # Taffy 0.15 brings:
    # 
    # - A new abspos model that enables hoisting in Taffy/Blitz, and more closely aligns with Servo's model.
    # - Support for the `normal` keyword for alignment properties
    # - Support for `fit-content(calc(...))`
    # - A number of misc. correctness fixes
    # - 60% faster CSS Grid layout
    # 
    # ### Abspos hoisting model
    # 
    # Older versions of Taffy didn't support hoisting absolute / fixed nodes above their immediate parent (it always treated the immediate parent as the containing block). We were (somewhat hackily) then using the computed position as the static position input to Servo's own abspos positioning, and implementing hoisting via a side channel in the integration code. Taffy 0.15 has changed this API, and now bubbles abspos nodes and computes a static position for us.
    # 
    # Most of the changes in this PR are adapting to the new API
    # 
    # ### Style conversion changes
    # 
    # - Taffy now supports the `position: static|fixed|sticky` keywords, so map them directly to their Taffy equivalents. Note: Taffy doesn't actually apply sticky positioning. The `sticky` keyword just makes containing block determination and suppressed `inset` application for `sticky` nodes.
    # - Taffy now supports the `normal` keyword for alignment properties so map it
    # - Taffy now support `fit-content(calc(...))` so map that
    # 
    # Testing: WPT tests
    #

.https://github.com/servo/servo/pull/49073	(@Loirooriol, #49073)	layout: Optimize flex column layout by caching default content block size (#49073)
    perf; speedup
    ^ commit f07470d3911ec462fb4d14fc55c11a0c30cbe80c
    # We already cache layout results, but only for the last size that we used to lay out. However, in a flex column layout, we were typically laying out the items multiple times: first with a `SizeConstraint::default()` block size, and later with the final block size. This meant that we lost the cache for `SizeConstraint::default()`, so if something changed and we needed a new layout, we weren't able to use the cache at all. This patch allows caching the default content block size separately. Therefore, we will be able to reuse that value even after laying out into the final size.
    # 
    # Testing: This should not change observable behavior, so no WPT tests should move, but it leads to an over 30% improvement in Speedometer 3.1 results.
